*For the Departed:* Give rest, O our Savior and Giver of life, unto our brethren whom Thou hast translated from temporal things, who cry: O Lord, glory to Thee.[^9291]

*Glory... Both now: Theotokion:* Daniel beheld Thee as the divine holy mountain, and wise Isaiah proclaimed Thee as Mother of God, O pure one, who didst contain in the flesh God, Who fashioned Adam: Whom, being incarnate, the Cherubim tremble before, singing and glorifying Him unceasingly with thrice-holy voices; and Him they entreat that our souls may be saved.[^9292]

*On Friday at Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Tone 7:*[^9293]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* At Thy nod the once fluid watery nature was changed into an earthly form, O Lord; therefore Israel, having journeyed on dry land, sings unto Thee a hymn of victory.[^9294]

*Refrain:* Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.[^9295]

*Troparion 1:* O Lady Theotokos, deliver those who have hope in Thee and flee unto Thee from afflictions and tribulations, and from falling, and from eternal fire and torment.[^9296]

*Troparion 2:* With the dew of Thy tender mercy, O pure Virgin, quench the flame of my downfalls, that I may not come unto the trial of all condemnation and future fire.[^9297]

*Glory:* Rain down upon me, O Virgin, drops of Thy tender mercy, who am ever dried up by the heat of passions, that I may unceasingly glorify God and our Savior Who was born of Thee.[^9298]

*Both now:* With the water of Thy prayer, O Virgin, bedew my soul which is inflamed with sorrowful heat, that I may bring forth fruits of divine gladness unto Him Who was born of Thee.[^9299]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* In the beginning, having established the heavens by Thy all-powerful Word, O Lord Savior, and all their host by the all-effective and divine Spirit: establish me upon the immovable rock of the confession of Thee.[^9300]

*Troparion 1:* Thee do I entreat, O Virgin: grant salvation and cleansing unto my soul, and ungrudging grace, as Thou art powerful and good, and as a maiden loving mankind, and vouchsafe me incorruptible life.[^9301]

*Troparion 2:* O unerring salvation and protectress of the world, who didst bear the Creator...[^9302]

...O pure Virgin: deliver from perils those who faithfully sing of Thee, and from the tribulations of life, and from eternal condemnation.[^9303]

*Glory:* We diligently pray unto Thy compassions, O Maiden: despise not Thy humble servants, but as Thou art good, look down with Thy merciful eye, and deliver us from the indissoluble tyranny of the devil.[^9304]

*Both now:* Now am I gripped by the violence of sorrow, O Virgin, having weakened all my strength, and I lie wholly prone upon the earth; yet I cry aloud unto Thee from my soul: Do Thou raise me up again, and strengthen me by Thy consolation.[^9305]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* Not having left the Father's bosom, and having come down to earth, O Christ God, I have heard the mystery of Thy dispensation, and I have glorified Thee, the only Lover of mankind.[^9306]

*Troparion 1:* O Mother of God, deliver me from corruption, and from the deceitful tormentor serpent who ever prowls around me and trapps my steps that are directed according to God.[^9307]

*Troparion 2:* O noetic portal of life, open unto me the doors of repentance; for now have I drawn near unto the doors of despair, wretched that I am by reason of my many sins.[^9308]

*Glory:* Having thoroughly shaken off the mire of passions and pleasures by Thy tender mercy, O all-holy one, show me forth pure, and clothe me with the light-fashioned garment of grace.[^9309]

*Both now:* Having cleansed the defilement of my soul and the uncleanness of my body, O pure one, by Thy acceptable supplications, present me pure ever to sing of Thee and glorify Thee.[^9310]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* Night is unilluminated unto the unbelieving, O Christ, but unto the faithful there is enlightenment in the sweetness of Thy words; therefore early in the morning do I rise unto Thee, and sing praise unto Thy Divinity.[^9311]

*Troparion 1:* Entreat the all-good Word, O all-immaculate Virgin, that we may be saved; for we have acquired Thee as a protectress and a mighty help in necessities.[^9312]

*Troparion 2:* Deliver me not up unto the enemies, O pure one, who vehemently seek my ruin by reason of my sins; but by Thy tender mercy snatch me from their harm.[^9313]

*Glory:* Knowing Thee to be the God of all, O Christ, and Creator of all, in Thee alone...[^9314]

...have we placed the hope of our salvation, Who for our sake didst become man.[^9315]

*Both now:* Rejoice, O pure cloud that pourest forth living water! Rejoice, O Virgin, the confirmation of the martyrs and of the apostles! Rejoice, honorable praise, O all-immaculate one![^9316]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Voyaging amid the turmoil of worldly cares, submerged with my ship by sins, and cast forth unto the soul-corrupting beast, like Jonah I cry aloud unto Thee, O Christ: Lead me up from the deadly deep![^9317]

*Troparion 1:* The comeliness and the beauty of excellence which I possessed through baptism have I destroyed by unseemly deeds, O Lady; yet do Thou by Thy fervent protection restore it unto those who faithfully honor Thee.[^9318]

*Troparion 2:* Deliver me from suffering and distressing constraint and grief, O Theotokos, granting me Thy consolation; for I have no other protectress save Thee, O all-immaculate one, I Thy servant.[^9319]

*Glory:* Having borne the Redeemer, the Benefactor, and the Savior, Thou hast strength and canst do whatever Thou desirest, O all-immaculate one; therefore do we Thy servants entreat Thee: Deliver us from passionate turmoil.[^9320]

*Both now:* Thee did the Prophet Isaiah of old behold as a light cloud, being enlightened by the Spirit, upon which the Lord of glory sat, and came, and cast down all the graven images of Egypt, O all-pure Virgin Mother.[^9321]

*Then, Lord, have mercy (forty times). Glory... Both now:*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 7:*[^9322]

Deliver from everlasting fire those who venerate Thy childbearing; for Christ hath shown Thee forth as a protectress unto us who sing of Thee.[^9323]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* The children of old showed the fiery furnace to be dew-dropping, hymning the one God and saying: The highly exalted and most glorious God of our fathers.[^9324]

*Troparion 1:* A multitude of sins holds me fast, and brings me down unto the corruption of ruin and despair. O Theotokos, anticipate me, and grant me saving help.[^9325]

*Troparion 2:* Deliver me from every malice of the enemy, and from temptation and affliction; and preserve my soul in stillness and peace, O all-pure one, that rejoicing I may sing of Thy power.[^9326]

*Glory:* Foreseeing the condemnation of my wicked deeds, wretch that I am, I cry unto Thee from the depths of my heart, O all-pure one: Be Thou my protection, O Lady, and save me.[^9327]

*Both now:* With the Martyrs, with the Angels, and with the Apostles, O all-immaculate one, entreat Thy Son and Lord to save Thy servants from assaults, perils, and griefs.[^9328]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Imitating the youths in the furnace, having received the spiritual dew, with faith we call out: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^9329]

*Troparion 1:* Be Thou a help unto me, O most holy Virgin, who flee unto Thee and cry aloud with faith: Have mercy, O pure one, and grant me cleansing of sins before the end; and at the hour of demise grant salvation, and after demise bestow rest.[^9330]

*Troparion 2:* Grievous dread takes hold of me when I ponder the fearful examination and the inexorable Judge of those who have wrought evil, such things as truly I, the wretched one, have wrought, and those torments that have no end. O Lady of the world, be Thou my salvation![^9331]

*Glory:* O Thou Who art the cause of salvation for those born of earth, O champion of the faithful, cleansing of sinners! O fountain of all goodness, and bestower of good things, O pure Parent of God: heal Thou the diseases of my soul, and pour forth eternal grace, and glory, and gladness.[^9332]

*Both now:* As an offering from us, who wast given unto the Master as from the beginning of nature, accept our supplications, O all-pure one, rendering unto us Thy help as fair gifts, that unceasingly we may hymn Him Who was born of Thee, and highly exalt Him unto the ages.[^9333]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* Mother of God and Virgin, having given birth and remaining a virgin still, this is not a work of nature, but of God's condescension; therefore as the only one accounted worthy of the marvels of God, Thee do we ever magnify.[^9334]

*Troparion 1:* Under Thy protection, O Virgin, have I now fled; tempest-tossed by a multitude of transgressions and perishing, save me, O Thou Who didst bear the Savior and Creator, and deliver me from eternal condemnation.[^9335]

*Troparion 2:* Entreat the Creator, God and Lord, Who was incarnate of Thy most pure blood, O pure one, on our behalf...[^9336]

...to have compassion in His tender mercy upon a despairing people.[^9337]

*Glory:* Deliver me from the unquenchable fire, and the outer darkness, and eternal weeping, who am worthy of condemnation, O most holy and pure Theotokos, by Thy fervent intercession unto God![^9338]

*Both now:* Preserved beneath Thy grace, O Theotokos, I remain undevoured by the assaults of the adversary enemies; therefore, as a divine foundation and ascent, I sing praise, and bow down, and magnify Thee.[^9339]

*It is truly meet. Trisagion. After the Our Father: troparia and the rest as usual.*[^9340]

*On Saturday at Matins.*[^9341]

*After the first Kathisma reading, Sessional Hymns to the Martyrs, Tone 7:*[^9342]

*Sessional Hymn 1:* O saints, pray that remission of our sins may be given unto us, and that we may be delivered from the grievous things that are expected, and from bitter death, we pray you.[^9343]

*Verse: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.*[^9344]

*Sessional Hymn 2:* Thy saints, O Lord, having struggled upon earth, trod down the enemy and abolished the deception of idols; therefore also did they receive crowns from Thee, the Master Who lovest mankind and the merciful God, Who bestowest great mercy upon the world.[^9345]

*Glory... Both now: Theotokion:* As the treasury of our resurrection, bring up from the pit and depth of transgressions those who trust in Thee, O all-hymned one; for Thou didst save those guilty of sin, having given birth unto our salvation, who wast a Virgin before childbearing, and a Virgin in childbearing, and after childbearing remainest still a Virgin.[^9346]

*After the second Kathisma reading, Sessional Hymns, Tone 7:*[^9347]

*Sessional Hymn 1:* Rejoice, ye righteous, and let the heavens be glad! For the martyrs, having struggled upon earth, trod down the enemy and abolished the deception of idols. Let the Church leap for joy, celebrating the victory unto the author of the contest and only author of victory, Christ God, Who bestows great mercy upon the world.[^9348]

*Verse: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.*[^9349]

*Sessional Hymn 2:* Thy martyrs, O Lord, overcame the enemy and put to shame the deception of idols, having armed themselves with the power of Thy Cross; therefore also...[^9350]

...singing with the Angels, they call out a song of victory, glorifying Thee, O Christ. By their prayers grant unto our souls cleansing and great mercy.[^9351]

*Verse: Many are the afflictions of the righteous, and out of them all the Lord will deliver them.*[^9352]

*Sessional Hymn 3 (Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ)):* Thy death, O Lord, caused life to bud forth unto the dead; for Thou didst despoil Hades, releasing those that slept in darkness. Therefore we beseech Thee, the Life-giver, as God: give rest with the righteous unto those whom Thou hast taken from us, that they may find great mercy at the judgment.[^9353]

*Glory... Both now: Theotokion:* O unwedded Maiden all-immaculate, entreat our God Who was incarnate of Thy blood ever with the Prophets, Hierarchs, and Martyrs, that our souls may be saved.[^9354]

*Canon to the Holy Martyrs, Hierarchs, Venerables, and the Departed.*[^9355]

*(Whose acrostic is: With choirs I sing of shepherds and martyrs, I, Joseph.)*[^9356]

*Tone 7. Ode 1. Heirmos:* Unto God Who aided Moses in Egypt to lead Israel forth, unto Him alone let us sing, for He is gloriously glorified.[^9357]

*Refrain: Holy Martyrs, pray to God for us.*[^9358]

*Troparion 1:* The choir of guileless martyrs, having overcome all the deceptions of the adversary, leaps with joy, rejoicing before the face of Him Who created it.[^9359]

*Refrain: Hierarchs of Christ, pray to God for us.*[^9360]

*Troparion 2:* Hierarchs of Christ, and all venerable ones who have fought the good fight, were accounted worthy of eternal delight by grace.[^9361]

*Refrain: All Saints, pray to God for us.*[^9362]

*Troparion 3:* Deliver us, O Christ, from all wrath, by the supplication of the Prophets, and Venerables, and holy women, and save our souls.[^9363]

*Refrain: Give rest, O Lord, to the souls of Thy departed servants.*[^9364]

*Troparion 4:* O Word, Who didst fashion me from the earth and command me to return unto the earth again: give rest unto those whom Thou hast translated in the faith.[^9365]

*Theotokion:* O all-pure Mother, who didst bear the most holy Word of God: sanctify all who glorify Thee with love.[^9366]

*Another Canon, to the Departed.*[^9367]

*(A work of Theophanes.)*[^9368]

*Tone 7. Ode 1. Heirmos:* At Thy nod the once fluid watery nature was changed into an earthly form, O Lord; therefore, having journeyed on dry land...[^9369]

...Israel, having journeyed on dry land, sings unto Thee a hymn of victory.[^9370]

*Verse: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.*[^9371]

*Troparion 1:* An adornment embellished with forms of virtue, and a divine dedicated offering radiant with diverse beauties in piety, O Martyrs, were ye offered unto Christ God.[^9372]

*Verse: Give rest, O Lord, to the souls of Thy departed servants.*[^9373]

*Troparion 2:* Vouchsafe Thy servants who have been translated, O most merciful Lord, to attain the radiance of the unapproachable and thrice-sunned dawn: whence all sickness, sorrow, and sighing have fled away.[^9374]

*Glory:* The nature condemned unto mortification, Thou Thyself, O Christ, being hypostatic Life and the Life-giver, didst raise up; therefore, as the only compassionate One, give rest unto those who have been translated in faith unto Thee.[^9375]

*Both now: Theotokion:* Thou didst set upright the ancestral mother's fall, O all-pure one, having given birth unto the Lord Word, Who raised her up and breathed life into those in the tombs by divine authority.[^9376]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Thou Who didst establish the heavens by Thy word, and strengthen the earth's foundation upon many waters: establish Thou my mind in Thy will, O Lover of mankind.[^9377]

*Refrain: Holy Martyrs, pray to God for us.*[^9378]

*Troparion 1:* Crushed with stones and cast into a pit, ye crushed all the power of the deceitful one, and unbroken in mind did ye remain, O Martyrs.[^9379]

*Refrain: Hierarchs of Christ, pray to God for us.*[^9380]

*Troparion 2:* With the radiance of divine teachings and the rays of virtues, the all-wise Hierarchs, enlightening the faithful, drove away all the mist of heresies.[^9381]

*Refrain: All Saints, pray to God for us.*[^9382]

*Troparion 3:* Having died unto the world, Thy venerable ones, O Word, inherited indeed life above the world; for their sake, O Christ, have compassion upon us all.[^9383]

*Refrain: Give rest, O Lord, to the souls of Thy departed servants.*[^9384]

*Troparion 4:* For those who have been translated in faith and hope, we all beseech the good Master to have mercy on them in the hour of judgment.[^9385]

*Glory... Both now: Theotokion:* Without emptying the Father's bosom, the Word appeared in Thy womb, O all-pure one, held as an Infant: the Beginningless One receiving a beginning from Thee.[^9386]

*Canon to the Departed. Ode 3. Heirmos:* In the beginning having established the heavens by Thy all-powerful Word, O Lord Savior, and all their host by the all-effective and divine Spirit: establish me upon the immovable rock of the confession of Thee.[^9387]

*Troparion 1:* Youthful and steadfast endurance the choirs of martyrs manfully showed forth; for they yielded up the onslaught of wounds and the stripes of torments, desiring Thine incorruptible glory and beauty, O Savior.[^9388]

*Troparion 2:* Into Thine eternal life receive the souls of those who have fallen asleep in hope, O Merciful One; and settle them within the longed-for and divine bosom of divine Abraham, O Master, and number them with Lazarus and Thine elect.[^9389]

*Glory:* Having come down from heaven to save the human race, O compassionate Savior: vouchsafe those who have piously been translated, by Thy tender mercy, to be filled with immaterial light, and Thy divine glory, and joy.[^9390]

*Both now: Theotokion:* The laws of nature were dissolved in Thee, who didst conceive the incomprehensible Word, O pure one; and the divine law was given unto us, granting remission of transgressions unto all who are in despair, by the grace of divine goodness.[^9391]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* Perceiving Thy coming in the flesh, O Christ, the Prophet Habakkuk cried aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^9392]

*Troparion 1:* Strengthened by love for the Lord, the Martyrs enfeebled the power of the enemy; therefore are they called blessed.[^9393]

*Troparion 2:* As sheep of the Shepherd and Lamb, O blessed Hierarchs, ye presided over the rational flock by divine grace.[^9394]

*Troparion 3:* Like stars of many lights, with the brilliance of virtue ye illuminated the fullness of the faithful, O our venerable Fathers.[^9395]

*Glory:* The unique triumph of the valiant women, and the assembly of the holy Prophets, have attained heavenly good things.[^9396]

*Both now: Theotokion:* Entreat God, Whom Thou didst bear, O all-hymned one, that Thy flock may be delivered from all temptations and afflictions.[^9397]

*Canon to the Departed. Ode 4. Heirmos:* Not having left the Father's bosom, and having come down to earth, O Christ God, I have heard the mystery...[^9398]

...of Thy dispensation, and I have glorified Thee, the only Lover of mankind.[^9399]

*Troparion 1:* Patiently displaying the pains of suffering, O Christ, the Martyrs were bound with the crowns of Thy righteousness, and they glorify Thy power.[^9400]

*Troparion 2:* Of Thy supernatural brilliance vouchsafe those who have been piously translated, when Thou shalt come in Thy glory with Thine Angels, O all-compassionate and most merciful One.[^9401]

*Glory:* Vouchsafe those who have departed before us and glorify Thee, O good One and Lover of mankind, to delight in the thrice-radiant dawn and the single shining of the Godhead.[^9402]

*Both now: Theotokion:* He Who left not the Father's bosom, Christ, took up His dwelling in the virginal womb, and hath delivered from death those who call Thee blessed, O Mother of God, only all-hymned one.[^9403]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* My spirit rises early unto Thee, O God, for Thou art light, and Thy commandments have become healing unto Thy servants, O Lover of mankind.[^9404]

*Troparion 1:* Ye took no thought for bodily wounds, O valiant sufferers, and by your divine wounds ye heal the wounds and sufferings of all.[^9405]

*Troparion 2:* Ye received the authority to bind and to loose upon earth, O Hierarchs of Christ; therefore loose our indissoluble bonds of sin.[^9406]

*Troparion 3:* Having been united unto God with a pure mind, the ascetic choirs, of the Prophets and Righteous, and of honorable women, make glad, rejoicing with an indestructible rejoicing.[^9407]

*Glory: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):* As the only one Who art most merciful, make those who in faith have been translated from this life, O Christ, citizens of Paradise, O Lover of mankind.[^9408]

*Both now: Theotokion:* Having been shown forth as an indestructible bridal chamber unto God, entreat Him, O all-holy Virgin, to make me an inhabitant of the noetic bridal chamber.[^9409]

*Canon to the Departed. Ode 5. Heirmos:* O Lord my God, rising early from the night I pray unto Thee: grant me remission of my transgressions, and direct my paths unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee.[^9410]

*Troparion 1:* Clothed brightly with life-bearing death, woven through martyrdom, all-praised Martyrs, entreat divine rest for the souls of those who have fallen asleep before...[^9411]

...us, entreat divine rest.[^9412]

*Troparion 2:* Pouring forth tender compassion from inexhaustible treasures, O Savior: be well pleased that the souls whom Thou hast translated may take up their dwelling with Thy firstborn in the heavenly habitations.[^9413]

*Glory:* Having put off heaviness and been loosed from bonds, and passed over unto the life on high, Thy servants, O Christ, vouchsafe, O Savior, to delight in Thy holy radiances.[^9414]

*Both now: Theotokion:* O Lady Mother of God, grant loosening of my transgressions, and bestow upon me forgiveness of my sins, O Thou who didst bear the hypostatic Life unto the world, O all-immaculate one.[^9415]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Jonah cried out from the belly of Hades: Lead my life up from corruption! And we cry aloud unto Thee: O almighty Savior, have mercy upon us![^9416]

*Troparion 1:* Ye cast down the haughtiness of the enemy, being lifted up unto God in sufferings, O all-glorious warriors, and became heavenly citizens.[^9417]

*Troparion 2:* Driving away the winter of heresies, the true Hierarchs of Christ brought a multitude of the pious unto the true spring.[^9418]

*Troparion 3:* By Thy strength, O Christ, the multitude of Venerables, and Prophets, and holy women were justified, and delight in the unsetting light.[^9419]

*Glory: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):* Thou hast translated Thy servants from temporal things, O compassionate One; yet vouchsafe them to be partakers of eternal gladness and true life.[^9420]

*Both now: Theotokion:* Sanctify Thy servants, O most holy Virgin, who didst bear the most holy Word in the flesh, Whom every breath hymns, sanctifying them.[^9421]

*Canon to the Departed. Ode 6. Heirmos:* Voyaging amid the turmoil of worldly cares, submerged with my ship by sins, and cast forth unto the soul-corrupting beast, like Jonah I cry aloud unto Thee, O Christ: Lead me up from the deadly deep![^9422]

*Troparion 1:* The choir of martyrs, having steadfastly endured intolerable pains, inherited painless delight, receiving the crowns of righteousness from the life-bearing right hand. *(Twice)*[^9423]

*Glory:* With the Righteous, where the angelic choirs are, and where the radiances of the saints and the enjoyment of eternal life abide...[^9424]

...rank Thy servants who have fallen asleep before us, O Merciful One, as Thou art immortal God.[^9425]

*Both now: Theotokion:* He Who by divine will and creative power composed all things out of non-being came forth from Thy womb, O pure one, and with God-originating brightness hath enlightened those that were in the darkness of death.[^9426]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* Being in the Chaldean furnace, the children of Abraham rejoiced with the Angel, saying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^9427]

*Troparion 1:* By the streams of their blood the passion-bearers quenched the flame of impiety, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^9428]

*Troparion 2:* We praise the Hierarchs who became the enlighteners of the world, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^9429]

*Troparion 3:* With songs let the holy assembly of the Prophets and the Venerables be honored, who sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^9430]

*Glory: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):* Deliver from Gehenna Thy faithful servants whom Thou hast received, O compassionate Christ, who cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^9431]

*Both now: Theotokion:* As more honorable than the Angels do we hymn the Theotokos, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^9432]

*Canon to the Departed. Ode 7. Heirmos:* The children showed the fiery furnace of old to be dew-dropping, hymning the one God and saying: The highly exalted and most glorious God of our fathers.[^9433]

*Troparion 1:* Wearing crowns, the choirs of martyrs clearly stand round Christ the King in mind, with the angelic choirs, calling out: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^9434]

*Troparion 2:* Thou Who hast authority over death and life, O compassionate One, make those who have been transferred in Thy faith, O Savior, partakers of divine delight, who call out: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^9435]

*Glory:* Enlightening them with the light of immaterial brilliance, O Christ, settle the souls whom Thou hast translated in the heavenly habitations, to glorify Thee unceasingly with Thy favored ones.[^9436]

*Both now: Theotokion:* He Who aforetime was without mother from the Father, now without father was ineffably born of Thee, O Virgin, Christ, having clothed Himself in flesh for the sake of us men: Blessed...[^9437]

...is the fruit of Thy womb, O all-pure one![^9438]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Imitating the youths in the furnace, having received the spiritual dew, with faith we call out: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^9439]

*Troparion 1:* Ye cast down the temples of idols, O all-glorious ones, and made yourselves temples unto the Spirit, O passion-bearers, having finished your course bravely.[^9440]

*Troparion 2:* Ye appeared as sweet-scented flowers, O Hierarchs, gladdening the souls of the faithful with the knowledge of piety; therefore are ye worthily called blessed.[^9441]

*Troparion 3:* Ye became sojourners on all the earth, O venerable ones, as Prophets of God, looking unto the inheritance on high and the ever-abiding glory.[^9442]

*Glory: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):* As Lord of the living and the dead, O Word, unite Thy servants who have fallen asleep in faith unto the choirs of all who are being saved, as the only Lover of mankind.[^9443]

*Both now: Theotokion:* The assembly of all women who sought the Lord by fasting and suffering prays unceasingly unto Thy divine person, O all-pure one.[^9444]

*Canon to the Departed. Ode 8. Heirmos:* The only beginningless King of glory, Whom the heavenly Powers bless, and before Whom the angelic ranks tremble: sing praise, ye priests, and highly exalt Him, ye people, unto all ages![^9445]

*Troparion 1:* Looking unto the heavenly glory of Christ's coming, the Martyrs took no thought for earthly glory, piously singing of Him as King unto all ages.[^9446]

*Troparion 2:* O Thou Who didst destroy the earthly tabernacle, grant a heavenly tabernacle unto those who have fallen asleep in hope of life, giving them rest in the habitations of the Righteous unto all ages.[^9447]

*Glory:* As God Who grantest resurrection unto the dead, Who hast the fountain of life: do Thou water those who have fallen asleep before us with torrents of delight, as the only good One, unto all ages.[^9448]

*Both now: Theotokion:* Having ineffably received the unapproachable Light in Thy womb, O Virgin Theotokos, Thou didst enlighten those who were in the darkness of this life, to glorify piously Christ Who ineffably came forth from Thee.[^9449]

*My soul doth magnify the Lord:*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* Mother beyond nature, and Virgin according to nature, the only blessed one among women, with hymns do we the faithful magnify the Theotokos.[^9450]

*Troparion 1:* By the prayers of the holy Martyrs, and of the Prophets and the Righteous, who from all ages have lived virtuously, O Christ, have mercy on our souls.[^9451]

*Troparion 2:* Having been shown forth as ministers unto the Master, stewards of sacred mysteries, ye were joined unto the heavenly servants; with them offer supplications for us.[^9452]

*Troparion 3:* Having finished the blessed course together with the women, let us honor the holy ascetic choirs, that by their prayers we may obtain sanctification.[^9453]

*Glory: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):* The glory of which the choirs of all the saints were accounted worthy, O Christ, vouchsafe those who have been translated, who served Thee in Orthodox manner, to partake of.[^9454]

*Both now: Theotokion:* Loving sin as I do, I tremble before the fearful judgment seat of Him Who was born of Thee, O pure one: wherein, as Thou art good, preserve me uncondemned.[^9455]

*Canon to the Departed. Ode 9. Heirmos:* Mother of God and Virgin, having given birth and remaining a virgin still, this is not a work of nature, but of God's condescension; therefore as the only one accounted worthy of the marvels of God, Thee do we ever magnify.[^9456]

*Troparion 1:* Manifestly the valiant martyrs enlighten the world, being pillars of the faith, an unshakable bulwark for the churches, and ramparts of piety; them do we the faithful worthily call blessed.[^9457]

*Troparion 2:* Deliver from eternal fire, O Master, those who have been translated from us, having torn asunder the bond of their sins with the spear of Thy side, O only Lover of mankind, and vouchsafe them the brightness of the Saints.[^9458]

*Glory:* As a good God, and Lover of mankind by nature, as merciful and compassionate, as the inexhaustible treasure of immortal life, O Savior: vouchsafe those who have fallen asleep before us in faith Thine incorruptible sweetness.[^9459]

*Both now: Theotokion:* The shadows of the Law and the ancient riddles have passed away through Thy childbearing; for Christ became the fulfillment of the Law and the Prophets; singing of Whom in two essences, Thee, the pure Ever-Virgin, do we call blessed.[^9460]

*It is truly meet. Litany, Photogogikon (Exapostilarion), and the customary psalms.*[^9461]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera to the Martyrs, Tone 7:*[^9462]

*Sticheron 1:* Celebrating the memory of Thy holy passion-bearers, Thee...[^9463]

...we praise, O Christ, crying aloud: O Lord, glory to Thee![^9464]

*Sticheron 2:* In the midst of the judgment hall of the transgressors of the law, the passion-bearers cried aloud with joy: O Lord, glory to Thee![^9465]

*Sticheron 3:* As luminaries have ye appeared unto the universe, O all-praised passion-bearers, crying aloud unto Christ: O Lord, glory to Thee![^9466]

*Sticheron 4:* Breathing as one and looking unto One, the passion-bearing Martyrs, having found the one path of life, which is death for Christ, striving with zeal one for the end of another—O the wonder!—as though snatching up treasures did they eagerly seize torments, saying one to another: For if we die not now, we shall die in any case, and we shall pay the debt due to our being; let us make necessity our honor, let us account what is common to all as our own, purchasing life with death. By their prayers, O God, have mercy on us.[^9467]

*Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):* According to Thine image and likeness, having created man in the beginning, Thou didst place him in Paradise to rule over Thy creatures; but being seduced by the envy of the devil, he partook of the food, becoming a transgressor of Thy commandments; therefore Thou didst condemn him, O Lord, to return again unto the earth from which he was taken, and to ask for rest.[^9468]

*Glory:* In the land of Thy Righteous, O Merciful One, do Thou appoint those who in faith have been translated from temporal things unto Thee, O Lover of mankind.[^9469]

*Both now: Theotokion:* Pray, O Virgin, with the Apostles and the Martyrs, that those who have been translated may find great mercy at the judgment.[^9470]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera to the Departed. Tone 7, Podoben: "Having disregarded temporal things" (Неради́вше):*[^9471]

*Sticheron 1:* Put to death wast Thou seen upon the Cross, O only Immortal One, and in the tomb wast Thou laid as one dead, delivering men from slaughter and mortal corruption; but as Thou art an inexhaustible deep of tender compassions and a fountain of goodness, do Thou give rest unto the souls of Thy servants who have been translated from us.[^9472]

*Verse:* Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken to Thyself, O Lord.[^9473]

*Sticheron 2:* With Thine incorruptible beauty, and with the sweetness of comeliness, and with the divine...[^9474]

...rays of Thy light, vouchsafe those who have been translated unto Thee, O Good One, to be enlightened, in the immaterial radiance of Thy manifest light, dancing with the Angels around Thee, the Master, and King of glory, and Lord.[^9475]

*Verse:* Their souls shall dwell amid good things.[^9476]

*Sticheron 3:* As Thou hast an inexhaustible magnificence of gifts, as an unfailing treasure of rich goodness, as God settle those who have been translated unto Thee in the lands of Thine elect: in a place of refreshment, in the house of Thy glory, in the delight of Paradise, in the virginal bridal chamber, as Thou art tender-hearted.[^9477]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:* The fulfillment of the Law didst thou bear, the Deliverer Who became flesh; for there was no justification for those who formerly were under the Law, but Christ was crucified for our sake and justified us. Therefore, having boldness as His Mother, pray Thy compassionate Son to give rest to the souls of those who have piously been translated from us, O all-hymned one.[^9478]

*On Saturday, The Beatitudes. Tone 7:*[^9479]

*Troparion 1:* Beautiful was the fruit, and good for food, that put me to death; Christ is the tree of life, eating of which I die not, but cry aloud with the thief: Remember me, O Lord, in Thy Kingdom.[^9480]

*Troparion 2:* Having finished the good fight, and having kept the faith, ye received incorruptible crowns from God, and were accounted worthy of His glory, O all-praised Martyrs, companions of the Angels.[^9481]

*Troparion 3:* Let the choirs of the Hierarchs, and the Venerable, and the Prophets, and the company of honorable women be sacredly honored, who now dwell in joy in the temples of the firstborn, and abide together with the bodiless ranks.[^9482]

*Troparion 4:* Appoint in the temples of the Saints those whom Thou hast translated from us, O Word of God, overlooking their spiritual sins committed in knowledge and in ignorance upon the earth, and have compassion on Thy servants.[^9483]

*Glory: To the Trinity:* Thy servants, O Holy Trinity, who...[^9484]

...dying, come unto Thee to be delivered from those dreadful torments, and asking to obtain the good things of Thy holy glory in the hour of judgment.[^9485]

*Both now: Theotokion:* The Father's radiance dwelt in thy womb, O all-holy and pure one, and renewed again those who were deceived by the evil counsel of the enemy and had fallen into corruption.[^9486]

*[Engraved Tailpiece: An ornamental baroque vignette with floral scrollwork and arabesques, marking the conclusion of Tone 7]*[^9487] *[Engraved Headpiece: An ornamental baroque headpiece frieze with medallions depicting the Annunciation, the Crucifixion of Christ, and the Mother of God with the Christ Child, amidst ornate arabesques and foliate scrollwork, marking the beginning of Tone 8]*[^9488]

---

### Notes

[^7954]: Physical Leaf p746 (Unnumbered, Printed Folio 736 / f. 734). Major Landmark: Opening of Tone 7 (Гласъ 7). Woodcut engraved headpiece depicting the Most Pure Virgin Theotokos with the Christ Child enthroned, flanked by prophets and angels within an ornate baroque frieze.
[^7955]: Physical Leaf p746. Saturday Little Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Resurrection Sticheron 1, Tone 7 (chanted twice).
[^7956]: Physical Leaf p746. Saturday Little Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Resurrection Sticheron 2, Tone 7.
[^7957]: Physical Leaf p746. Saturday Little Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Resurrection Sticheron 3, Tone 7.
[^7958]: Physical Leaf p746. Saturday Little Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Glory... both now... Theotokion (Dogmatikon of Little Vespers), Tone 7: an extended dogmatic formulation of the two natures and single Hypostasis of Christ.
[^7959]: Physical Leaf p746. Little Vespers rubrics: Phos Hilaron ("O Gladsome Light"), Saturday Evening Prokeimenon "The Lord is King" (LXX Ps. 92:1), chanted three times, followed by the prayer "Vouchsafe, O Lord."
[^7960]: Physical Leaf p746. Little Vespers Aposticha, Resurrection Sticheron, Tone 7.
[^7961]: Physical Leaf p747 (Printed Folio 735, misprinted for 737; signature 94). Little Vespers Aposticha, Verse 1: LXX Ps. 44:18 (Masoretic Ps. 45:17).
[^7962]: Physical Leaf p747. Little Vespers Aposticha, Sticheron 1 to the Theotokos, Tone 7, modeled upon the Podoben "Having disregarded temporal things" (Неради́вше ѡ҆ вре́менныхъ).
[^7963]: Physical Leaf p747. Little Vespers Aposticha, Verse 2: LXX Ps. 44:11 (Masoretic Ps. 45:10).
[^7964]: Physical Leaf p747. Little Vespers Aposticha, Sticheron 2 to the Theotokos, Tone 7 (Jesse's rod prophecy, Isaiah 11:1).
[^7965]: Physical Leaf p747. Little Vespers Aposticha, Verse 3: LXX Ps. 44:13 (Masoretic Ps. 45:12).
[^7966]: Physical Leaf p747. Little Vespers Aposticha, Sticheron 3 to the Theotokos, Tone 7 (The Gate of Ezekiel, Ezek. 44:1-2).
[^7967]: Physical Leaf p747. Little Vespers Aposticha, Glory... both now... Theotokion, Tone 7, affirming the duality of natures in the one Christ.
[^7968]: Physical Leaf p747. Concluding Little Vespers rubrics: Nunc Dimittis ("Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart"), Trisagion prayers, Our Father, Resurrectional Troparion of Tone 7 ("Thou didst destroy death by Thy Cross"), Resurrectional Theotokion ("As the treasure of our resurrection"), Litany, and Dismissal.
[^7969]: Physical Leaf p748 (Unnumbered, Printed Folio 738 / f. 736). Opening of Saturday Great Vespers, Tone 7. Attributed rubric: hymnographic composition of St. John of Damascus (Творе́нїе І҆ѡа́нна Дамаскина̀). Ten stichoi appointed for "Lord, I have cried."
[^7970]: Physical Leaf p748. Saturday Great Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Resurrection Sticheron 1, Tone 7 (St. John of Damascus).
[^7971]: Physical Leaf p748. Saturday Great Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Resurrection Sticheron 2, Tone 7 (St. John of Damascus).
[^7972]: Physical Leaf p748. Saturday Great Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Resurrection Sticheron 3, Tone 7 (St. John of Damascus).
[^7973]: Physical Leaf p748. Saturday Great Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Sticheron 4 (Anatolian Sticheron 1), Tone 7.
[^7974]: Physical Leaf p748. Saturday Great Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Sticheron 5 (Anatolian Sticheron 2), Tone 7.
[^7975]: Physical Leaf p748. Saturday Great Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Sticheron 6 (Anatolian Sticheron 3), Tone 7, opening clause bridging across leaves p748 and p749.
[^7976]: Physical Leaf p749 (Printed Folio 737, misprinted for 739). Saturday Great Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Sticheron 6 conclusion.
[^7977]: Physical Leaf p749. Saturday Great Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Sticheron 7 (Anatolian Sticheron 4), Tone 7.
[^7978]: Physical Leaf p749. Rubrics for 3 Stichera to the Most Holy Theotokos authored by Paul of Amorium (Па́ѵла А҆морре́йскагѡ), chanted in Tone 7 to the Podoben "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва).
[^7979]: Physical Leaf p749. Great Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Sticheron 8 (Theotokion 1 by Paul of Amorium).
[^7980]: Physical Leaf p749. Great Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Sticheron 9 (Theotokion 2 by Paul of Amorium).
[^7981]: Physical Leaf p749. Great Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Sticheron 10 (Theotokion 3 by Paul of Amorium).
[^7982]: Physical Leaf p749. Great Vespers, Lord, I have cried, Glory... both now... Theotokion (The Great Dogmatikon of Tone 7: Ма́ти ꙋ҆́бѡ позна́ласѧ є҆сѝ, па́че є҆стества̀ Бцⷣе).
[^7983]: Physical Leaf p749. Great Vespers rubrics: Entrance with the censer, Phos Hilaron, Saturday Evening Prokeimenon, and standard litanies.
[^7984]: Physical Leaf p749. Great Vespers Aposticha, Resurrection Sticheron 1, Tone 7.
[^7985]: Physical Leaf p749. Great Vespers Aposticha, Verse 1: LXX Ps. 92:1a (Masoretic Ps. 93:1a).
[^7986]: Physical Leaf p749. Great Vespers Aposticha, Sticheron 2 (Alphabetical Sticheron 1), Tone 7.
[^7987]: Physical Leaf p749. Great Vespers Aposticha, Verse 2: LXX Ps. 92:1b (Masoretic Ps. 93:1b).
[^7988]: Physical Leaf p750 (Printed Folio 738, misprinted for 740). Great Vespers Aposticha, Sticheron 3 (Alphabetical Sticheron 2), Tone 7.
[^7989]: Physical Leaf p750. Great Vespers Aposticha, Verse 3: LXX Ps. 92:5 (Masoretic Ps. 93:5).
[^7990]: Physical Leaf p750. Great Vespers Aposticha, Sticheron 4 (Alphabetical Sticheron 3), Tone 7.
[^7991]: Physical Leaf p750. Great Vespers Aposticha, Glory... both now... Theotokion, Tone 7.
[^7992]: Physical Leaf p750. Concluding Great Vespers rubrics: Nunc Dimittis, Trisagion, Our Father, and Resurrectional Troparion of Tone 7 (Разрꙋши́лъ є҆сѝ кресто́мъ твои́мъ сме́рть).
[^7993]: Physical Leaf p750. Great Vespers, Glory... both now... Resurrectional Theotokion of Tone 7 (Ꙗ҆́кѡ на́шегѡ воскресе́нїа сокро́вище).
[^7994]: Physical Leaf p750. Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Tone 7, Ode 1, Heirmos (Сокрꙋши́вшемꙋ бра̑ни мы́шцею свое́ю). Reference to the drowning of Pharaoh's charioteers (Exod. 15).
[^7995]: Physical Leaf p750. Compline Canon, Ode 1 liturgical refrain to the Mother of God.
[^7996]: Physical Leaf p750. Compline Canon, Tone 7, Ode 1, Troparion 1.
[^7997]: Physical Leaf p750. Compline Canon, Tone 7, Ode 1, Troparion 2.
[^7998]: Physical Leaf p750. Compline Canon, Tone 7, Ode 1, Troparion 3.
[^7999]: Psalm 44:14 LXX: "All the glory of the king's daughter is within, clothed around with golden fringes, variously adorned."
[^8000]: Psalm 32:6 LXX: "By the Word of the Lord the heavens were established, and all the host of them by the Spirit of His mouth."
[^8001]: Slavonic: "ꙗ҆́кѡ ѿ нача́ла на́мъ весе́лїю" (Greek: ὡς ἀρχηγῷ τῆς ἡμῶν χαρᾶς) — as unto the wellspring and cause of our joy.
[^8002]: Slavonic: "Ма́ти безневѣ́стнаѧ" (Greek: Μῆτερ ἀνύμφευτε).
[^8003]: Slavonic: "И҆змѣне́нїе прегрѣше́нїй и҆ напа́стей" — relief or alteration and turning away of sins and perils.
[^8004]: Slavonic: "присножи́зненное на́мъ благода́нїе" — perpetual living beneficence and gift of grace unto us.
[^8005]: Habakkuk 3:3 LXX: "His virtue covered the heavens, and the earth was full of His praise."
[^8006]: Slavonic: "пѣ́снь ра́дованїѧ" — referring to the Angelic Salutation ("Rejoice!").
[^8007]: Slavonic: "дꙋше́вныма рꙋка́ма" (with hands of the soul), an Eastern hymnographic metaphor for spiritual offering.
[^8008]: Slavonic: "стра́стнїи грѣсѝ" — sinful passions.
[^8009]: Slavonic: "Блаже́ни вои́стинꙋ чтꙋ́щїи тѧ̀" — Blessed truly are they who revere thee.
[^8010]: Isaiah 26:9 LXX: "With my soul have I desired Thee in the night; yea, with my spirit within me will I rise early at dawn unto Thee, O God."
[^8011]: The first troparion of Ode 5 carries over onto physical leaf p752.
[^8012]: Completion of Ode 5, Troparion 1 from physical leaf p751.
[^8013]: Slavonic: "весе́лїѧ наде́ждꙋ тѧ̀ ѡ҆брѣто́хомъ" — we found thee to be our hope of joy.
[^8014]: Slavonic: "храни́лище" — storehouse, treasury, safe-keeping place.
[^8015]: Slavonic: "недꙋ́гъ лю́тыхъ" — cruel and severe diseases.
[^8016]: Jonah 2:2, 7 LXX: "Out of the belly of hades I cried... Let my life come up from corruption, O Lord my God."
[^8017]: Slavonic: "ра́дованїе Гаврїи́лово" — Gabriel's "Rejoice" (the Archangelic salutation).
[^8018]: Slavonic: "Въ глꙋбинꙋ̀ печа́ли и҆ ѡ҆бстоѧ́нїѧ" — into the depth of sorrow and distress.
[^8019]: Slavonic: "Подъ до́лгомъ содѣ́ласа ве́сь мі́ръ" — the whole world hath become indebted.
[^8020]: Slavonic: "ꙗ҆́вѣ и҆ вта́й" — openly and in secret or privately.
[^8021]: The standard mesodion / midpoint rubric at Compline following Ode 6.
[^8022]: Psalm 78:13 LXX: "So we Thy people and the sheep of Thy pasture will give Thee thanks for ever."
[^8023]: Daniel 3:26, 50 LXX: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers... and made the midst of the furnace as it had been a moist whistling wind."
[^8024]: Luke 1:42: "Blessed art thou among women, and blessed is the fruit of thy womb."
[^8025]: Psalm 29:6 LXX: "In the evening weeping may tarry, but rejoicing comes in the morning."
[^8026]: Slavonic: "и҆змѣне́нїе" — transformation, relief, reversal of afflictions.
[^8027]: Slavonic: "бꙋ́ри и҆ тѧ́жкихъ печа́лей" — the storm and grievous griefs.
[^8028]: Daniel 3:57–86 LXX: "O all ye works of the Lord, bless ye the Lord: praise and supremely exalt Him unto all ages."
[^8029]: Slavonic: "ѿ печа́ли въ ра́дость преше́дше" — having passed from sorrow into joy (cf. John 16:20).
[^8030]: Slavonic: "мольбꙋ̀ хꙋдꙋ́ю" — humble and poor supplication.
[^8031]: Slavonic: "Прегрѣше́нїй разрꙋше́нїа вина̀" — O cause of the dissolution and pardon of sins.
[^8032]: Slavonic: "ѿ тебе́ бо прїе́млемъ Хрⷭᵎтїа́не ми́лость твою̀" — for from Thee do we Christians receive Thy mercy.
[^8033]: Luke 1:28, 48: "Blessed art thou among women... for, behold, from henceforth all generations shall call me blessed."
[^8034]: Ode 9, Troparion 1 concludes on physical leaf p754.
[^8035]: Ode 9, Troparion 1 concludes from physical leaf p753.
[^8036]: Slavonic: "Дѣ́во неискꙋсомꙋ́жнаѧ" (Greek: Παρθένε ἀπειρόγαμε) — Virgin unwedded and that knewest not man.
[^8037]: Psalm 115:8 LXX: "I will offer to Thee the sacrifice of praise, and will call upon the name of the Lord."
[^8038]: Slavonic: "бж҃е́ственномꙋ Рождествꙋ̀" — at thy divine childbearing.
[^8039]: Conclusion of Saturday Compline (Apodeipnon) with standard dismissal prayers.
[^8040]: Sunday Midnight Office (Mesonyktikon), containing the Canon to the Holy Trinity by Metrophanes of Smyrna (9th c.).
[^8041]: The Slavonic acrostic reads: "Хвалю̀ Трⷪце, тѧ̀ є҆динонача́льное є҆стество̀" (I praise the Trinity, the monarchical nature).
[^8042]: Exodus 14:21–22; 15:1. Heirmos of Ode 1, Tone 7.
[^8043]: Canon refrain addressed directly to the All-Holy Trinity.
[^8044]: Slavonic: "ᲂу҆ста̀ ᲂу҆́мнаѧ се́рдца" — the spiritual and noetic mouth of the heart.
[^8045]: Genesis 1:26–27: "Let us make man according to our image and likeness."
[^8046]: Classical Patristic Trinitarian terminology: Mind (Nous/Father), Word (Logos/Son), and Spirit (Pneuma/Holy Spirit).
[^8047]: Exodus 3:2; the unburnt bush as a theological type of the Incarnation and the Virgin Birth.
[^8048]: Psalm 32:6 LXX. Ode 3 Heirmos continues onto physical leaf p755.
[^8049]: Completion of Ode 3 Heirmos from physical leaf p754.
[^8050]: Slavonic: "безмѣ́рноси́льный Бж҃е" — O God of immeasurable power.
[^8051]: Slavonic: "Ѡ҆́расль... сра́сленымъ" (Greek: Ἔρνος... συμφυεῖ) — the theological botanical metaphor of the shoot and branch co-sprouting from the root of the Father.
[^8052]: Slavonic: "нераздѣ́льнѣ и҆ раздѣ́льнѣ" (Greek: ἀδιαιρέτως καὶ διῃρημένως) — classic Patristic formula of the Trinity: divided in Persons, united in essence.
[^8053]: Slavonic: "Бг҃онача́лїе трисїѧ́нное и҆ є҆ди́нственное сꙋщество́мъ" — the Godhead thrice-radiant and single in essence.
[^8054]: Standard Kathisma and Sessional rubrical pause after Ode 3.
[^8055]: Podoben incipit canonical English: "He Who for my sake" (И҆́же менѐ ра́ди).
[^8056]: Slavonic: "ᲂу҆блажѝ" — sweeten, soothe, make blessed.
[^8057]: Habakkuk 3:2 LXX. Ode 4 Heirmos.
[^8058]: Slavonic: "Содержи́тельнаѧ трисвѣ́тлаѧ Е҆ди́нице" — O all-holding thrice-radiant Monad and Unity.
[^8059]: Slavonic: "Назна́менательныхъ рече́нїй... недоꙋмѣва́юще" — being in perplexity before the symbolic expressions of Thine ineffable Divinity.
[^8060]: Slavonic: "нераздѣ́лнѡ тѧ̀ раздѣлѧ́емъ" — undividedly do we divide Thee (referring to the three distinct Hypostases in one undivided Essence).
[^8061]: Theotokion of Ode 4 carries over onto physical leaf p756.
[^8062]: Conclusion of Ode 4 Theotokion from physical leaf p755.
[^8063]: Isaiah 26:9 LXX. Ode 5 Heirmos.
[^8064]: Slavonic: "Трⷪце начерта́ньми" (Greek: Τριὰς τοῖς χαρακτῆρσι) — Trinity in characters or hypostatic properties.
[^8065]: Slavonic: "трисо́лнечное є҆стество̀" (Greek: τρισηλίῳ φύσει) — thrice-sunned nature and Godhead.
[^8066]: Slavonic: "сообра́знꙋ и҆ сопресто́льнꙋ" — equal in form and enthroned together.
[^8067]: Jonah 2:3–7 LXX. Ode 6 Heirmos.
[^8068]: Classical Cappadocian Trinitarian dogma: the divine Persons differ solely in their hypostatic properties while sharing identically the one essence.
[^8069]: Slavonic: "бре́нными ᲂу҆сты̑" — with clay and mortal lips.
[^8070]: Slavonic: "ѿ со́лнца О҆ц҃а̀, и҆́но со́лнце пре́жде вѣ̑къ" — the Father as the unoriginate Sun, and the Son as another Sun begotten before the ages.
[^8071]: Podoben incipit canonical English: "Brighter than fire" (О҆гнѧ̀ свѣтлѣ́йши).
[^8072]: Theotokion continues onto physical leaf p757.
[^8073]: Conclusion of Sessional Theotokion from physical leaf p756.
[^8074]: Daniel 3:26, 50 LXX. Ode 7 Heirmos.
[^8075]: Slavonic: "Свѣтолꙋ́чными блиста́нїи" — with beam-flashing splendors.
[^8076]: Slavonic: "въ трїе́хъ начерта́нїихъ" — in three characters or hypostatic representations.
[^8077]: Slavonic: "съ невеще́ственными чи́ньми" (Greek: σὺν ταῖς ἀΰλοις τάξεσι) — with the immaterial and bodiless ranks of the angels.
[^8078]: Luke 1:42: "Blessed art thou among women, and blessed is the fruit of thy womb."
[^8079]: Exodus 3:2; 4:10; Daniel 3:57. Moses "slow of speech and stammering" (медленоѧзы́чномꙋ и҆ гꙋгни́вомꙋ, Greek: βραδυγλώσσῳ καὶ μογιλάλῳ).
[^8080]: Slavonic: "ѡ҆зари́тисѧ свѣтодѣ́тельными сподо́би" — vouchsafe to be illumined by light-working rays.
[^8081]: Slavonic: "привлади́ма" (привлека́ема) — drawn and attracted by love.
[^8082]: Troparion 3 of Ode 8 concludes on physical leaf p758.
[^8083]: Conclusion of Ode 8, Troparion 3 from physical leaf p757.
[^8084]: Daniel 3:57 LXX. The earth "as another heaven" singing the Benedicite hymn.
[^8085]: Ode 9 Heirmos, Tone 7: celebrating the Virgin's virginity in and after childbirth as a miracle of divine condescension.
[^8086]: Slavonic: "Высокосло́вити и҆ пѣ́ти тѧ̀ досто́йнѡ" — to speak high theological words and sing worthily.
[^8087]: Slavonic: "дꙋше́внагѡ пристра́стїа" — soulful and spiritual impassioned attachment.
[^8088]: Slavonic: "неизмѣ́ннаѧ добро́то сꙋ́щественныѧ бла́гости" — unchangeable beauty of essential goodness.
[^8089]: 2 Peter 1:4: "that by these ye might be partakers of the divine nature." Patristic theosis formula.
[^8090]: The Megalynaria and Trinity refrains composed by St. Gregory of Sinai (14th c.), sung at the conclusion of the Sunday Midnight Office.
[^8091]: Beginning of Sunday Matins (Orthros) for Tone 7.
[^8092]: Psalm 117:27 LXX: "God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us." Rubric for Troparia and Kathisma readings.
[^8093]: John 20:19; Psalm 50:12 LXX. Resurrectional Troparion and Kathisma 1 hymn of Tone 7.
[^8094]: LXX Psalm 9:33 (Masoretic Ps. 9:12 / 10:12).
[^8095]: Sessional hymn continues onto physical leaf p759.
[^8096]: Conclusion of the First Kathisma Sessional hymn from physical leaf p758.
[^8097]: Slavonic: "на безпо́мѡщныѧ лю́ди ᲂу҆милосе́рдисѧ" — have compassion upon the helpless people.
[^8098]: Matthew 27:66; 28:2–6. Second Kathisma Sessional hymn.
[^8099]: LXX Psalm 9:2.
[^8100]: Mark 16:3; Isaiah 9:6 LXX: "Angel of Great Counsel" (Μεγάλης Βουλῆς Ἄγγελος / вели́ка совѣ́та А҆́гглъ).
[^8101]: Conclusion of Second Kathisma hymn on right column.
[^8102]: Second Kathisma Theotokion.
[^8103]: Philippians 2:7: "took upon Him the form of a servant, and was made in the likeness of men." The Hypakoe of Sunday Tone 7.
[^8104]: The Anabathmoi (Songs of Ascents, Gradual Psalms) based on Psalms 119–133 LXX.
[^8105]: Psalm 125:1 LXX: "When the Lord turned back the captivity of Zion, we became as comforted ones."
[^8106]: Psalm 125:4–6 LXX: "Turn back, O Lord, our captivity, as the streams in the south. They that sow in tears shall reap in joy."
[^8107]: Isaiah 11:2 LXX: "The spirit of wisdom and understanding, the spirit of counsel and might, the spirit of knowledge and piety... the spirit of the fear of God."
[^8108]: Standard Anabathmoi rubric: the Triadikon sticheron on "Glory" is repeated on "Both now".
[^8109]: Psalm 126:1 LXX: "Except the Lord build the house, they labor in vain that build it."
[^8110]: Psalm 126:3 LXX: "Lo, sons are an heritage of the Lord: the fruit of the womb is his reward."
[^8111]: Patristic Triadikon on the Divinity of the Holy Spirit as source of being and unapproachable light (1 Timothy 6:16).
[^8112]: Psalm 127:1 LXX: "Blessed are all they that fear the Lord, that walk in His ways."
[^8113]: Psalm 127:3 LXX: "Thy wife shall be as a fruitful vine by the sides of thine house: thy children like olive plants round about thy table." 1 Peter 5:4: "Chief Shepherd" (Chief Pastor / пастыренача́льникъ).
[^8114]: Romans 11:33: "O the depth of the riches both of the wisdom and knowledge of God! how unsearchable are His judgments!"
[^8115]: LXX Psalm 9:33 (Masoretic Ps. 9:12 / 10:12). Sunday Matins Prokeimenon, Tone 7.
[^8116]: LXX Psalm 9:2. Stichoi for the Matins Prokeimenon.
[^8117]: Psalm 150:6: "Let every breath praise the Lord."
[^8118]: Matins Gospel order: "Let every breath," Stichos from Psalm 150:1, reading of the designated Eothinon Resurrection Gospel per the Typikon cycle, chanting of "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ," and Psalm 50.
[^8119]: Exodus 14:21–22. The primary Resurrectional Heirmos of Tone 7 (composed by St. John of Damascus).
[^8120]: The canon refrain for the Resurrectional Canon: "Сла́ва Гдⷭᵎи, ст҃о́мꙋ воскресе́нїю твоемꙋ̀."
[^8121]: John 12:31: "now shall the prince of this world be cast out." The Tree of the Cross overturning the tree of the Fall.
[^8122]: Acts 2:24: "having loosed the pangs of death: because it was not possible that He should be holden of it."
[^8123]: Genesis 3:16 overturned by the virginal childbirth of the Theotokos without travailing pangs.
[^8124]: Exodus 15:1, 3–4. Heirmos of the secondary Canon of the Cross and Resurrection (composed by St. Cosmas of Maiuma).
[^8125]: Sunday Matins, Tone 7: Refrain for the Canon of the Precious and Life-Creating Cross and Resurrection (composed by St. Cosmas the Melodist).
[^8126]: Ode 1 Troparion 1: John 19:34. The two life-bearing streams of blood and water flowing from Christ's pierced side, fulfilling the typology of the water from the rock and the sacrificial blood.
[^8127]: Ode 1 Troparion 2: 1 Corinthians 15:53–54. Christ's bodily descent into the tomb and third-day resurrection imparting the hope of incorruption unto mortal humanity.
[^8128]: Ode 1 Theotokion: Luke 1:28. The virginal childbirth preserved intact after the birth of the incarnate Creator.
[^8129]: Liturgical heading: The secondary Parakletike Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos in Tone 7, ascribed to St. John of Damascus.
[^8130]: Ode 1 Heirmos incipit of the Theotokos Canon, referring to the crossing of the Red Sea (Exodus 14).
[^8131]: Canon refrain: "Most Holy Lady Mother of God, save us" (Прест҃а́ѧ Гпⷭᵎжѐ Бцⷣе, спасѝ на́съ).
[^8132]: Ode 1 Troparion 1: The Theotokos as the vessel of the abyss of divine mercy and tender compassion.
[^8133]: Ode 1 Troparion 2: Christ the divine Physician healing human nature wounded by the serpent's arrow of sin.
[^8134]: Ode 1 Troparion 3: Romans 5:12–17. Mortal human nature, bound by death through the ancestral fall, restored unto incorruptible life through the conception of the Life Himself.
[^8135]: Rubric for the festal Katavasia: the Heirmos of the Theotokos Canon ("I will open my mouth", Tone 4).
[^8136]: Ode 3 Heirmos: Psalm 32:6 LXX: "By the word of the Lord the heavens were established; and all the host of them by the breath of His mouth."
[^8137]: Ode 3 Troparion 1: Colossians 1:20–22; 2 Corinthians 5:18–19. Christ reconciling the world unto the Father through the suffering and wounds of the Cross.
[^8138]: Ode 3 Troparion 2: Genesis 3:15; Acts 2:24. Christ's descent into hades by the power of the Cross to heal the spiritual venom of the serpent.
[^8139]: Ode 3 Theotokion: Codicological witness of the 1895 Lviv Stauropegion edition, providing the diplomatic petition for the reigning sovereign: "І҆мпера́торꙋ (И҆́мкꙋ) побѣ́дꙋ да́рꙋй на ва́рвары врагѝ" ("grant victory unto the Emperor [Name] over barbarian enemies"), commemorating Apostolic King Franz Joseph I of Austria-Hungary.
[^8140]: Canon of Cross & Resurrection, Ode 3 Heirmos: Psalm 103:2–5 LXX. The establishment of heaven and earth by divine volition as a type of the steadfastness of faith in Christ.
[^8141]: Ode 3 Troparion 1: Luke 23:43. Christ opening Paradise unto the penitent thief through the voluntary passion of the Cross.
[^8142]: Ode 3 Troparion 2: John 11:25. Christ the Giver of life rising on the third day and shining upon creation.
[^8143]: Ode 3 Theotokion: Genesis 3:16. The virginal conception of the Word delivering Eve from the curse.
[^8144]: Ode 3 Heirmos incipit of the Theotokos Canon, repeating the theme of the creation of the heavens.
[^8145]: Ode 3 Troparion 1: Genesis 3:4–5. The deceit of the serpent who promised deification through transgression, overturned by the divine condescension of the incarnate Word.
[^8146]: Ode 3 Troparion 2: Luke 1:42. Elizabeth's salutation to the Mother of God, whose fruit dispels ancestral sorrow.
[^8147]: Ode 3 Troparion 3: Ephesians 2:14–16. Christ as our peace reconciling humanity unto the Father through the mediation of the Virgin.
[^8148]: Ode 4 Heirmos: Habakkuk 3:2 LXX: "O Lord, I have heard Thy report, and was afraid; O Lord, I considered Thy works, and was astonished."
[^8149]: Ode 4 Troparion 1: Isaiah 50:6: "I gave My back to scourges, and My cheeks to blows." The sinless Master suffering stripes in place of the guilty servant.
[^8150]: Ode 4 Troparion 2: John 18:22; Matthew 26:67. The Judge of all standing before the earthly tribunal and struck by a hand of clay.
[^8151]: Ode 4 Theotokion: The Virgin Mother guiding the faithful into the spiritual haven of salvation (sentence bridges to physical leaf p763).
[^8152]: Continuation of Ode 4 Theotokion from leaf p762: "приста́нищꙋ, Всенепоро́чнаѧ, сла́внагѡ є҆гѡ̀ хотѣ́нїа" (haven of His glorious will).
[^8153]: Canon of Cross & Resurrection, Ode 4 Heirmos: Habakkuk 3:13 LXX: "Thou wentest forth for the salvation of Thy people, to save Thine anointed."
[^8154]: Ode 4 Troparion 1: 2 Corinthians 5:21; Hebrews 2:14. Christ, Who knew no sin, assuming human flesh to destroy the devil through death.
[^8155]: Ode 4 Troparion 2: Colossians 2:14. Christ lifted up upon the Cross to cancel the debt of Adam's transgression.
[^8156]: Ode 4 Theotokion: Christ's bodily death overcoming death and reviving Adam who had fallen through delusion.
[^8157]: Ode 4 Heirmos incipit of the Theotokos Canon: "Ѻ҆́тча нѣ́дра не ѡ҆ста́вль" (Leaving not the Father's bosom).
[^8158]: Ode 4 Troparion 1: Song of Songs 4:7: "Thou art all fair, My love; there is no spot in thee." The Virgin chosen in the divine counsel before all creation.
[^8159]: Ode 4 Troparion 2: The Mother of God bearing the Word incarnate from her pure blood, granting remission of sins.
[^8160]: Ode 4 Troparion 3: Rational human and angelic nature offering liturgical and sacerdotal praise unto Christ for the miracle of the Virgin birth.
[^8161]: Ode 5 Heirmos: Isaiah 26:9 LXX: "With my soul have I desired Thee in the night; yea, with my spirit within me will I seek Thee early."
[^8162]: Ode 5 Troparion 1: Zechariah 11:12; Matthew 26:15; Micah 5:1. Christ betrayed for thirty pieces of silver and smitten upon the cheek to procure divine freedom.
[^8163]: Ode 5 Troparion 2: 1 Corinthians 1:25. The divine strength manifested through the weakness of the flesh, triumphing over death.
[^8164]: Ode 5 Theotokion: Luke 1:34–35. The virgin birth accomplished by the overshadowing of the Holy Spirit without human fatherhood.
[^8165]: Canon of Cross & Resurrection, Ode 5 Heirmos: Isaiah 26:9 LXX. The soul's early dawn vigil seeking divine enlightenment and forgiveness.
[^8166]: Ode 5 Troparion 1: Matthew 27:45, 51; Mark 15:38. The darkening of the sun and renting of the temple veil at Golgotha testifying to the end of the Old Covenant.
[^8167]: Ode 5 Troparion 2: Hebrews 2:14. Christ destroying the tyrant's kingdom by His almighty Godhead and raising the dead.
[^8168]: Ode 5 Theotokion: Supplication to the Theotokos for cleansing of sins and divine intercession.
[^8169]: Ode 5 Heirmos incipit of the Theotokos Canon: "Но́щь несвѣ́тла невѣ́рнымъ" (Night without light is unto the unbelieving).
[^8170]: Ode 5 Troparion 1: Genesis 28:12. Jacob's ladder connecting heaven and earth as a prophetic prefiguration of the Incarnation through the Theotokos.
[^8171]: Ode 5 Troparion 2 (part 1): The faithful receiving eternal redemption through the Mother of God (text bridges to physical leaf p765).
[^8172]: Ode 5 Troparion 2 (part 2): Continuation from leaf p764: Gabriel's greeting ("Rejoice, O Bride of God") offered in spiritual joy.
[^8173]: Ode 5 Troparion 3: Song of Songs 2:2: "As the lily among thorns, so is my love among the daughters." The heavenly Bridegroom choosing the all-pure Virgin.
[^8174]: Ode 6 Heirmos: Jonah 2:3–7 LXX. The prophet Jonah swallowed by the sea monster as a type of the soul tossed on the turbulent sea of life.
[^8175]: Ode 6 Troparion 1: 1 Peter 3:19–20; 4:6. Christ preaching deliverance and salvation unto the spirits held in hades.
[^8176]: Ode 6 Troparion 2: John 2:19–22. The temple of Christ's body destroyed by the Passion and rebuilt on the third day, transforming apostolic sorrow into triumph.
[^8177]: Ode 6 Theotokion (part 1): The ineffable mystery of the Virgin's virginal conception and childbirth (text bridges to physical leaf p765).
[^8178]: Continuation of Ode 6 Theotokion from leaf p764: "кто̀ ѿ человѣ́къ сказа́ти возмо́жетъ?" (who among men is able to recount? The uncircumscribed Word united unto flesh).
[^8179]: Canon of Cross & Resurrection, Ode 6 Heirmos: Jonah 2:2 LXX: "I cried by reason of mine affliction unto the Lord, and He heard me."
[^8180]: Ode 6 Troparion 1: Matthew 27:51. The shaking of the earth at the crucifixion of the Master, healing the wounds of creation.
[^8181]: Ode 6 Troparion 2: 1 Corinthians 15:20–22. Christ opening a path of life and resurrection for mankind through His triumph over hades.
[^8182]: Ode 6 Theotokion: The Theotokos as the steadfast hope and protectress of all faithful believers.
[^8183]: Ode 6 Heirmos incipit of the Theotokos Canon: "Пла́ваѧ въ молвѣ̀ жите́йскихъ" (Sailing in the surge of worldly cares).
[^8184]: Ode 6 Troparion 1: Hebrews 1:1. The Old Testament figures and prophecies prefiguring the incarnation of the universal Benefactor.
[^8185]: Ode 6 Troparion 2 (part 1): Adam exiled from the delight of Paradise through the serpent's malice (text bridges to right column of leaf p765).
[^8186]: Ode 6 Troparion 2 (part 2): The restoration of Adam and fallen humanity through the virgin birth of the Redeemer.
[^8187]: Ode 6 Troparion 3: Colossians 1:16; John 1:4–5. The Creator of all things illuminating the gloom of death with the lightning flashes of His Divinity.
[^8188]: Sunday Resurrectional Kontakion, Tone 7: The destruction of the power of death and the liberation of hades, summoning the faithful to enter into the resurrection.
[^8189]: Sunday Resurrectional Ikos, Tone 7: Hades and death trembling before One of the Trinity; the Tree of the Cross restoring Adam and his descendants to Paradise.
[^8190]: Ode 7 Heirmos: Daniel 3:49–50; Song of the Three Holy Children. The fiery furnace of Babylon transformed into dropping dew by the presence of the Angel of the Lord.
[^8191]: Ode 7 Troparion 1: Romans 5:12–19. The disobedience of Adam at the tree of Eden overturned by the voluntary obedience and crucifixion of the Son of God.
[^8192]: Ode 7 Troparion 2: Christ rising from the tomb as the radiant light blossoming in hades and imparting life to the dead.
[^8193]: Ode 7 Theotokion: The Virgin Mary addressed as daughter of Adam, through whom corruption was vanquished and salvation revealed.
[^8194]: Canon of Cross & Resurrection, Ode 7 Heirmos: Daniel 3:26, 52 LXX. The Three Youths praising the God of our fathers in the midst of the flames.
[^8195]: Ode 7 Troparion 1 (part 1): Colossians 2:14. Christ blunting the sting of sin and tearing up the handwriting of human debt with the soldier's spear (text bridges to leaf p767).
[^8196]: Ode 7 Troparion 1 (part 2): The biblical refrain of Ode 7: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers."
[^8197]: Ode 7 Troparion 2: Hebrews 9:13–14; John 19:34. The divine blood from Christ's pierced side cleansing the earth defiled by the blood of pagan sacrifices.
[^8198]: Ode 7 Triadikon: Orthodox Trinitarian theology confessing one undivided Divine Essence in three distinct Hypostases: Father, Word, and Holy Spirit.
[^8199]: Ode 7 Heirmos incipit of the Theotokos Canon: "Пе́щь ѻ҆́трѡцы ѻ҆гнепа́льнꙋ дре́вле" (Of old the Children showed forth the fiery furnace).
[^8200]: Ode 7 Troparion 1: Psalm 44:10, 14 LXX. The Queen arrayed in golden vesture, loved and honored by her Creator and Lord.
[^8201]: Ode 7 Troparion 2: Isaiah 6:6–7. The live coal of the Seraph touching the prophet's lips prefiguring the divine fire borne in the Virgin's womb.
[^8202]: Ode 7 Troparion 3: The ancient prophets rejoicing in the symbolic visions of the mystery of the virgin birth (text bridges to physical leaf p767).
[^8203]: Continuation of Ode 7 Troparion 3 from leaf p766: "ва́хꙋ: превозноси́мый ѻ҆тце́въ Бг҃ъ, и҆ препросла́вленъ" (crying aloud: The supremely exalted and supremely glorified God of our fathers).
[^8204]: Ode 8 Heirmos: Exodus 3:2; Daniel 3:57. The burning bush on Mount Sinai and the Three Youths in the furnace revealing the mystery of God without consuming human weakness.
[^8205]: Ode 8 Troparion 1: Hebrews 9:11–12; 10:1–14. Christ the rational Lamb slain for the sins of the world, replacing the shadow of Mosaic animal sacrifices.
[^8206]: Ode 8 Troparion 2: 1 Corinthians 15:42–44. Human flesh, once subject to corruption, made incorruptible and glorified through the Passion and Resurrection of the Savior.
[^8207]: Ode 8 Theotokion: The purity of the Mother of God sanctifying the defilement of creation and reconciling humanity unto God.
[^8208]: Canon of Cross & Resurrection, Ode 8 Heirmos: Daniel 3:57–90. The angelic choirs and all created works exhorted to supremely exalt the unoriginate King of glory.
[^8209]: Ode 8 Troparion 1: Christ's voluntary crucifixion and shattering of the power of hades.
[^8210]: Ode 8 Troparion 2: Hebrews 2:14–15. The abolition of death's dominion and the glorious rising from the tomb.
[^8211]: Ode 8 Theotokion: The pre-eternal Word born of the Virgin Mary, loosing the ancient curse of Eve.
[^8212]: Ode 8 Heirmos incipit of the Theotokos Canon: "Неѡпа́льнаѧ ѻ҆гню̀ въ Сїна́и прича́щшаѧсѧ" (The bush unconsumed by fire that had communion on Sinai).
[^8213]: Ode 8 Troparion 1: The light of the Nativity illuminating the world, as the Virgin holds the uncontainable God in her arms (text bridges to physical leaf p768).
[^8214]: Continuation of Ode 8 Troparion 1 from leaf p767: The liturgical refrain of the Benedicite: "All ye works of the Lord, sing the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages."
[^8215]: Ode 8 Troparion 2: The womb of the Virgin containing the uncircumscribed God and enlightening the faithful with divine knowledge.
[^8216]: Ode 8 Troparion 3: The Mother of God as the dwelling-place of light, transfiguring believers into children of divine illumination.
[^8217]: Sunday Matins rubric: The Canticle of the Theotokos (Magnificat, Luke 1:46–55) with the megalynarion "More honorable than the Cherubim" chanted before Ode 9.
[^8218]: Ode 9 Heirmos: The Virgin Mary lending flesh unto the divine Word, becoming the spiritual vessel of the uncontainable Creator.
[^8219]: Ode 9 Troparion 1: Patristic christological defense against Theopaschism: Christ suffered in the flesh on the Cross while remaining impassible in His Divine nature.
[^8220]: Ode 9 Troparion 2: 1 Corinthians 15:12–20. The empty tomb of Christ as incontrovertible proof and earnest of the universal physical resurrection.
[^8221]: Ode 9 Triadikon: Classical Trinitarian refutation of Sabellian modalism (confusing the Persons) and Arian subordinationism (dividing the Essence).
[^8222]: Canon of Cross & Resurrection, Ode 9 Heirmos: The paradox of virginal childbirth surpassing the laws of nature through the condescension of God.
[^8223]: Ode 9 Troparion 1: Nicene Creed; Hebrews 1:3. Christ as Light from Light and the radiance of the Father's glory dispelling spiritual darkness.
[^8224]: Ode 9 Troparion 2: Council of Chalcedon (451 AD). Refutation of Monophysitism: Christ possessing two natures without confusion, truly dying as man and rising as God.
[^8225]: Ode 9 Troparion 3: Mark 16:1–6; Luke 24:5. The angel at the sepulcher directing the myrrh-bearing women to offer hymns to the living God rather than myrrh for a corpse.
[^8226]: Ode 9 Theotokion: Psalm 44:11–12 LXX. The Church gathered from the Gentile nations confessing Christ as the only God and Bridegroom.
[^8227]: Rubric: Ode 9 Heirmos of the Theotokos Canon sung to the melody of the preceding canon.
[^8228]: Ode 9 Troparion 1: John 4:24. The Theotokos as the mediatress of eternal joy, giving birth to the Redeemer Who delivers all who worship Him in spirit and truth.
[^8229]: Ode 9 Troparion 2: Psalm 131:8 LXX. King David prophetically identifying the Mother of God as the spiritual Ark of holiness holding the pre-eternal Word.
[^8230]: Ode 9 Troparion 3 (part 1): The Virgin exalted above all creation, bearing the Creator in the flesh (text bridges to right column of leaf p769).
[^8231]: Ode 9 Troparion 3 (part 2): The Mother of God bearing victory over all adversaries as Mother of the supreme Master.
[^8232]: Post-Canon Matins rubrics: The Little Litany, the hymn "Holy is the Lord our God" (Psalm 98:9 LXX), and the Morning Exapostilarion according to the appointed Sunday Eothinon Gospel cycle.
[^8233]: Matins Praises (Lauds), Tone 7: Psalm 148:1–4; Psalm 150:1. The Resurrectional Sticheron announcing the shattering of death and cosmic joy in heaven and earth.
[^8234]: Praises Sticheron 2: The ancient resurrectional hymn of devotion: worshipping the holy and sinless Lord Jesus Christ.
[^8235]: Praises Sticheron 3: Ceaseless veneration of the third-day Resurrection as the divine victory over iniquity and death.
[^8236]: Praises Sticheron 4 (part 1): Psalm 115:3 LXX; John 1:14; Malachi 4:2. The recapitulation of the Incarnation: the Word becoming flesh, acting as Benefactor, Liberator, and Sun of righteousness (text bridges to physical leaf p770).
[^8237]: Praises Sticheron 4 (part 2): Doxological conclusion: "Къ немꙋ́же возопїи́мъ: Бж҃е на́шъ, сла́ва тебѣ̀" (unto Whom let us cry aloud: O our God, glory to Thee!).
[^8238]: Anatolian (Eastern) Sticheron 1, Tone 7: Christ shattering the gates of hades and raising those who slept in darkness from the beginning of the world.
[^8239]: Anatolian Sticheron 2, Tone 7: The summons to rejoice in the Lord for freeing souls from the unloosed bonds of hades and granting eternal life.
[^8240]: Matins Praises Stichos: Psalm 9:33 LXX (Masoretic 9:12 / 10:12): "Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up; forget not Thy poor unto the end."
[^8241]: Anatolian Sticheron 3, Tone 7: Matthew 28:2–7; Mark 16:5–7. The angel shining on the stone of the tomb, commanding the women to tell the disciples that Christ goes before them into Galilee.
[^8242]: Matins Praises Stichos: Psalm 9:2 LXX: "I will give thanks unto Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart; I will tell of all Thy marvelous works."
[^8243]: Anatolian Sticheron 4, Tone 7: Psalm 117:22; Daniel 2:34–35, 45; Daniel 7:13–14; Numbers 20:11. Comprehensive prophetic typology: the rejected cornerstone, the rock flowing with water in the desert, the stone cut from the mountain without hands, and the Son of Man coming on the clouds to receive an everlasting kingdom.
[^8244]: Matins concluding rubrics: Doxasticon on "Glory" from the 11 Evangelical Stichera, "Both now" with the Theotokion "Most blessed art thou", chanting of the Great Doxology, and the Sunday Resurrectional Troparion "Today salvation is come unto the world" (Дне́сь спасе́нїе мі́рꙋ бы́сть).
[^8245]: Liturgical rubric opening the Divine Liturgy: The Beatitudes (Matthew 5:3–12) in Tone 7 with appointed Resurrectional troparia.
[^8246]: Liturgy Beatitudes Troparion 1: Genesis 2:9, 17; 3:6; Luke 23:42. The contrast between the deadly fruit of Eden and Christ the true Tree of Life, joined with the cry of the penitent thief.
[^8247]: Liturgy Beatitudes Troparion 2: Colossians 2:14. Christ lifted up upon the Cross to blot out the handwriting of Adam's ancient transgression (sentence bridges to physical leaf p771).
[^8248]: Physical leaf p771 (printed fol. 771, ѱѻ҃а). Continuation of Troparion 2 of the Sunday Beatitudes (Tone 7) from p770: "рукописа́ніе дре́внягѡ греха̀ А҆да́м... / ...ха̀ загла́дилъ є҆сѝ, и҆ спа́слъ є҆сѝ ѿ пре́лести ве́сь ро́дъ человѣ́ческій; тѣ́мже воспѣва́емъ тѧ̀, благодѣ́телю Гдⷭ҇и" (Thou hast blotted out the handwriting of Adam's ancient sin, and hast saved the whole human race from deception; therefore we sing unto Thee, O Benefactor and Lord).
[^8249]: Physical leaf p771. Troparion 3 of the Sunday Beatitudes (Tone 7): contrasting the venom of the serpent poured into Eve's hearing with the sweetness of eternal life poured forth into the world from Christ's pierced side upon the Tree of the Cross.
[^8250]: Physical leaf p771. Troparion 4 of the Sunday Beatitudes (Tone 7): Christ laid in the tomb as mortal yet breaking the iron bars of hades as almighty God and enlightening all by His third-day resurrection.
[^8251]: Physical leaf p771. Troparion 5 of the Sunday Beatitudes (Tone 7): the resurrection of Christ raising Adam and restoring the ancient incorruption of paradise; concluding with the prayer of the wise thief (Luke 23:42).
[^8252]: Physical leaf p771. Troparion 6 of the Sunday Beatitudes (Tone 7): Christ's post-resurrection bestowal of peace upon the disciples and the apostolic commission to bring all nations into His kingdom (cf. Matt. 28:18–20; John 20:19–21).
[^8253]: Physical leaf p771. Triadikon (Glory...): orthodox confession of the Holy Trinity as one Light in three Hypostases (Father, Son, and Holy Spirit), undivided and unconfused in one Divine nature and principle.
[^8254]: Physical leaf p771. Theotokion (Both now...): the ineffable incarnation of the pre-eternal Word from the Virgin Mother for the deification (theosis) of mankind and the salvation of Christians.
[^8255]: Physical leaf p771. Codicological and liturgical landmark: opening of the weekly liturgical cycle of Monday, dedicated to the Holy Bodiless Powers of Heaven (Archangels, Angels, and the celestial hierarchies).
[^8256]: Physical leaf p771. Rubric: Sunday Evening Vespers (Въ Недѣ́лю ве́чера), which initiates the liturgical day of Monday in the Byzantine rite.
[^8257]: Physical leaf p771. Rubric: Penitential Stichera on "Lord, I have cried" in Tone 7 (На Гдⷭ҇и воззва́хъ: стїхи̑ры покаѧ́нны. Гла́съ з҃).
[^8258]: Physical leaf p771. Melodic model heading: *Tone 7, Podoben: "Today Judas watches" (Дне́сь бди́тъ І҆ꙋ́да):*, registered under canonical identifier POD.T7.DNES_BDIT_IUDA.
[^8259]: Physical leaf p771. Sticheron 1 on "Lord, I have cried" (opens on p771, concluding on p772): compunctionate contemplation of the soul calling upon the divine condescension (kenosis) whereby God bowed the heavens and descended to raise man from the mire of passions unto the rock of faith.
[^8260]: Physical leaf p772 (printed fol. 772, ѱѻ҃в). Conclusion of Sticheron 1 on "Lord, I have cried" from p771: "...ry to Thy condescension, O Lover of mankind!" (Сла́ва и҆стоща́нію твоемꙋ̀, Человѣколю́бче).
[^8261]: Physical leaf p772. Sticheron 2 on "Lord, I have cried": dramatic rhetorical examination of conscience; the sinner marvelling that the earth has not swallowed him nor beasts devoured him, calling for immediate repentance before the Lord.
[^8262]: Physical leaf p772. Sticheron 3 on "Lord, I have cried": petition for deliverance from spiritual adversity, worldly turmoil, and passion, praying to find Christ a clement and merciful Master on the Day of Judgment.
[^8263]: Physical leaf p772. Rubric: Stichera to the Saint of the day from the Menaion; in their absence, the following three stichera to the Holy Bodiless Angels are appointed.
[^8264]: Physical leaf p772. Melodic model heading: *Tone 7, Podoben: "No longer are we forbidden" (Не ктомꙋ̀ возбрани́ми):*, registered under canonical identifier POD.T7.NE_KTOMU_VOZBRANENI.
[^8265]: Physical leaf p772. Sticheron 1 to the Bodiless Angels: petition unto the Holy Archangels, who are enlightened by the uncreated rays of the Trinity, to enlighten the faithful.
[^8266]: Physical leaf p772. Sticheron 2 to the Bodiless Angels: the celestial powers characterized as "secondary lights" (свѣ́ти вторі́и) by participation in the Primary Light (свѣ́тъ пе́рвый), reflecting Dionysian angelology (De Coelesti Hierarchia).
[^8267]: Physical leaf p772. Sticheron 3 to the Bodiless Angels: asking the angelic shelter and protection against demonic darkness and the spirits of malice.
[^8268]: Physical leaf p772. Theotokion (Glory... Both now...): praising the Ever-Virgin Mother who nursed the Redeemer with milk and delivered the world from demonic deception.
[^8269]: Physical leaf p772. Rubric: chanting of the evening hymn "O Gladsome Light" (Свѣ́те ти́хїй), the daily Prokeimenon of Sunday evening, and the customary litanies.
[^8270]: Physical leaf p772. Rubric: Penitential Stichera at the Aposticha in Tone 7 (На Стїхо́внѣ, стїхи̑ры покаѧ́нны. Гла́съ з҃).
[^8271]: Physical leaf p772. Sticheron 1 at the Aposticha: returning unto the Father like the prodigal son (Luke 15:11–32) and pleading to be received as one of His hired servants.
[^8272]: Physical leaf p772. Scriptural verse: Psalm 122:1–2 (LXX versification; Masoretic Ps. 123:1–2): "Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes..."
[^8273]: Physical leaf p772. Sticheron 2 at the Aposticha: spiritual allegorization of the Parable of the Good Samaritan (Luke 10:30–37); the soul wounded by robber-passions fleeing unto the heavenly Physician.
[^8274]: Physical leaf p772. Scriptural verse (opens on p772, concluding on p773): Psalm 122:3–4 (LXX versification; Masoretic Ps. 123:3–4): "Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us..."
[^8275]: Physical leaf p773 (printed fol. 773, ѱѻ҃г). Conclusion of Scriptural verse from p772: "...our soul with the reproach of them that are at ease, and with the contempt of the proud" [LXX Ps. 122:4].
[^8276]: Physical leaf p773. Sticheron 3 at the Aposticha (Martyrion/Apostolicum): glorifying Christ as the praise of the Apostles and crown of the Holy Martyrs who confessed the consubstantial Trinity.
[^8277]: Physical leaf p773. Theotokion (Glory... Both now...): Marian greeting ("Rejoice!") uniting classic Old Testament types: bridal chamber, heavenly portal, fiery throne of the Cherubim, unhewn mountain of Daniel, and the burning bush of Sinai.
[^8278]: Physical leaf p773. Rubric: Canticle of Simeon (Ны́нѣ ѿпꙋща́еши / Nunc Dimittis, Luke 2:29–32), Trisagion prayers, Troparia, and standard dismissal of Vespers.
[^8279]: Physical leaf p773. Rubric: Small Compline on Sunday evening (Въ Недѣ́лю на Повече́ріи), preceding Monday Matins.
[^8280]: Physical leaf p773. Rubric: Supplicatory Canon to the Most Holy Mother of God in Tone 7 (Канѡ́нъ моле́бный прест҃ѣ́й Бцⷣѣ. Гла́съ з҃).
[^8281]: Physical leaf p773. Ode 1. Heirmos: the miraculous crossing of the Red Sea by Israel dry-shod when the fluid waters solidified by divine command (Exod. 14:21–29).
[^8282]: Physical leaf p773. Liturgical refrain: "Most Holy Lady Mother of God, save us" (Прест҃а́ѧ Гпⷭ҇жѐ Бцⷣе, спасѝ на́съ).
